# Pri<sub>o</sub>rit<sub>y</sub>FEC<sub>:</sub> D<sub>ea</sub>dlin<sub>e-</sub>A<sub>wa</sub>r<sub>e</sub> Str<sub>ea</sub>min<sub>g</sub> FEC f<sub>o</sub>r KV<sub>-</sub>C<sub>ac</sub>h<sub>e</sub> H<sub>a</sub>nd<sub>o</sub>f in Ed<sub>ge</sub> M<sub>u</sub>lti<sub>-</sub>A<sub>ge</sub>nt S<sub>ys</sub>t<sub>e</sub>m<sub>s</sub>

Han Shen, Qifu T<sub>y</sub>ler Sun, Member, IEEE, Fan<sub>y</sub>an<sub>g</sub> Men<sub>g</sub>, Member, IEEE, Ye Wan<sub>g</sub> and Yon<sub>g</sub>shen<sub>g</sub> Lian<sub>g</sub>

Abstract—Multi-agent large language model (LLM) systems are increasingly deployed at the edge to support complex tasks that require collaboration and information exchange among multiple agents. However, when task contexts are passed across agents, shared context that has already been processed by upstream agents often needs to be prefilled again, resulting in substantial redundant computation. Reusing an already prefilled key–value (KV) cache can avoid this recomputation, but requires the reusable KV state to be transferred across the network before the receiving agent can begin generation. Under strict time-tofirst-token (TTFT) deadlines and unreliable edge links, delivering the complete KV cache may be infeasible, making it necessary to prioritize and protect the most valuable KV information within a limited transmission budget. This paper proposes PriorityFEC, a utility-aware KV-cache handof scheme that jointly exploits model-side KV importance and transport-side reliability. PriorityFEC first constructs a utility-aware source stream by placing high-utility KV blocks at earlier transmission positions. It then protects the ordered stream using a feedback-adaptive Streaming Forward Erasure Correction (FEC) mechanism, which schedules repair packets according to receiver feedback before the TTFT deadline. We implement PriorityFEC on a distributed edge LLM serving prototype and evaluate it with Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct and Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct across multiple datasets. Results show that PriorityFEC achieves superior task quality over the compared transport schemes, with its advantages becoming particularly pronounced under stringent deadline constraints. These results demonstrate the importance of jointly optimizing KV-cache utility and deadline-aware transport reliability when reusable LLM state must be handed of across unreliable edge networks.

Index Terms—Large language model serving, KV cache, collaborative inference, KV-cache handof, Streaming FEC, deadlineaware transport.

At the trans<sub>p</sub>ort la<sub>y</sub>er, QUIC-FEC [8] inte<sub>g</sub>rates several erasure codes into QUIC for proactive loss recover<sub>y</sub>, and AC-RLNC [24] uses dela<sub>y</sub>ed acknowled<sub>g</sub>ments to causall<sub>y</sub> <sub>a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t <sub>co</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>ons</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d <sub>erasure process.</sub> FlEC [25] further ex<sub>p</sub>oses a<sub>pp</sub>lication-aware reliabilit<sub>y</sub> and re<sub>p</sub>airschedulin<sub>g</sub> mechanisms within QUIC. ## B<sub>.</sub> M<sub>app</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> KV Utilit<sub>y</sub> t<sub>o</sub> T<sub>ranspor</sub>t Bl<sub>oc</sub>k<sub>s</sub>

L<sub>e</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pac</sub>k<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>ze</sub>d KV <sub>cac</sub>h<sub>e</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ranspor</sub>t<sub>-</sub>bl<sub>oc</sub>k <sub>se</sub>t

$$
B = \{ 1 , \ldots , B \} .\tag{1}
$$

Each block contains m fixed-size source packets,

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathcal { P } _ { b } = \{ \mathbf { s } _ { b , 1 } , \dotsc , \mathbf { s } _ { b , m } \} , \qquad b \in \mathcal { B } . } \end{array}\tag{2}
$$

Th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l bl<sub>oc</sub>k i<sub>s</sub> <sub>pa</sub>dd<sub>e</sub>d <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> <sub>necessary</sub> <sub>so</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>a</sub>ll t<sub>ranspor</sub>t bl<sub>oc</sub>k<sub>s</sub> h<sub>ave equa</sub>l <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ze.</sub> P<sub>a</sub>ddi<sub>ng sym</sub>b<sub>o</sub>l<sub>s carry zero u</sub>tilit<sub>y.</sub>

Let C denote the set of <sub>p</sub>rofiled la<sub>y</sub>er–<sub>p</sub>osition cells and let $\widehat { u } _ { c } \geq 0$ d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e measure</sub>d <sub>sens</sub>iti<sub>v</sub>it<sub>y o</sub>f $c \in { \mathcal { C } } .$ <sub>.</sub> B<sub>ecause</sub> <sub>pro</sub>fili<sub>ng-ce</sub>ll <sub>an</sub>d <sub>pac</sub>k<sub>e</sub>t b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>i<sub>es nee</sub>d <sub>no</sub>t <sub>co</sub>i<sub>nc</sub>id<sub>e,</sub> l<sub>e</sub>t

$\alpha _ { i , c } \in [ 0 , 1 ]$ denote the fraction of cell c carried b<sub>y</sub> source <sub>p</sub>ac<sup>k</sup>et $i ,$ <sub>w</sub>ith

$$
\sum _ { i } \alpha _ { i , c } = 1 , \qquad \forall c \in { \mathcal { C } } .\tag{3}
$$

Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pac</sub>k<sub>e</sub>t<sub>-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>u</sub>tilit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s</sub>

$$
v _ { i } = \sum _ { c \in \mathcal { C } } \alpha _ { i , c } \widehat { u } _ { c } ,\tag{4}
$$

<sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e correspon</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> bl<sub>oc</sub>k <sub>u</sub>tilit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s</sub>

$$
U _ { b } = \sum _ { i \in \mathcal { P } _ { b } } v _ { i } .\tag{5}
$$

$$
{ \mathcal { A } } = \{ a ^ { \mathrm { s } } \} \cup \{ a ^ { \mathrm { r } } ( k ) : k \in K \} ,\tag{15}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $a ^ { \mathrm { s } }$ continues source transmission, K is a set of discrete <sub>num</sub>b<sub>ers an</sub>d $a ^ { \mathrm { r } } ( k )$ transmits a burst of k repair packets. A <sub>source ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>ransm</sub>it<sub>s up</sub> t<sub>o</sub> $M _ { \mathrm { s } }$ <sub>consecu</sub>ti<sub>ve source pac</sub>k<sub>e</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>cross</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e curren</sub>t KV<sub>-</sub>bl<sub>oc</sub>k b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ary.</sub>

Let B denote the set of KV blocks, $U _ { b }$ th<sub>e u</sub>tilit<sub>y o</sub>f bl<sub>oc</sub>k $b ,$ <sub>an</sub>d $Z _ { b } ( t ) \ \in \ \{ 0 , 1 \}$ indicate whether block b has been completel<sub>y</sub> recovered b<sub>y</sub> epoch t. The normalized utilit<sub>y</sub> <sub>ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e a</sub>t th<sub>e rece</sub>i<sub>ver</sub> i<sub>s</sub>

$$
J _ { t } ^ { \mathrm { K V } } = \frac { \displaystyle \sum _ { b \in \mathcal { B } } U _ { b } Z _ { b } ( t ) } { \displaystyle \sum _ { b \in \mathcal { B } } U _ { b } } .\tag{16}
$$

The scheduling objective is therefore

$$
\operatorname* { m a x } _ { \pi } \quad \mathbb { E } _ { \pi } \left[ J _ { t _ { \mathrm { e n d } } } ^ { \mathrm { K V } } \right] ,\tag{17a}
$$

$$
\mathrm { s . t . } \quad u _ { t } \in \mathcal { A } _ { t } ,\tag{17b}
$$

$$
\sum _ { t } c _ { \mathrm { r e p } } ( u _ { t } ) \leq B ^ { \mathrm { r e p } } ,\tag{17c}
$$

$$
\sum _ { t } c _ { \mathrm { s l o t } } ( u _ { t } ) \leq T ^ { \mathrm { m a x } } ,\tag{17d}
$$

We therefore emplo<sub>y</sub> Double DQN to l<sub>earn</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>repa</sub>i<sub>r-sc</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d<sub>u</sub>li<sub>ng</sub> <sub>po</sub>li<sub>cy.</sub> A<sub>s</sub> ill<sub>us</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> Fi<sub>g.</sub> $^ { 4 , }$ the trainin<sub>g</sub> procedure maintains an online Q-network $Q _ { \theta }$ f<sub>or</sub> <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>va</sub>l<sub>ue</sub> <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> t<sub>oge</sub>th<sub>er</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>a</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t Q-network $Q _ { \bar { \theta } }$ f<sub>or</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

B<sub>o</sub>th <sub>ne</sub>t<sub>wor</sub>k<sub>s s</sub>h<sub>are</sub> th<sub>e same</sub> li<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ure.</sub> Given the 15-dimensional normalized state, the Q-network <sub>cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>s o</sub>f t<sub>wo</sub> f<sub>u</sub>ll<sub>y connec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d hidd<sub>en</sub> l<sub>ayers w</sub>ith 64 R<sub>e</sub>LU <sub>un</sub>it<sub>s eac</sub>h<sub>,</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>owe</sub>d b<sub>y a</sub> f<sub>our-</sub>di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>ona</sub>l li<sub>near ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t l<sub>ayer</sub> <sub>correspon</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e sc</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d<sub>u</sub>li<sub>ng ac</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub>

D<sub>ur</sub>i<sub>ng o</sub>fli<sub>ne</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng,</sub> th<sub>e sc</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d<sub>u</sub>l<sub>er</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erac</sub>t<sub>s w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> Streaming FEC environment using an ϵ-greed<sub>y</sub> polic<sub>y</sub> over th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>eas</sub>ibl<sub>e ac</sub>ti<sub>on se</sub>t<sub>.</sub> E<sub>ac</sub>h <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d t<sub>rans</sub>iti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s s</sub>t<sub>ore</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> <sup>the</sup> <sup>re</sup>p<sup>la</sup>y <sup>memor</sup>y <sup>as</sup>

$$
e _ { t } = ( { \bf x } _ { t } , u _ { t } , r _ { t } , { \bf x } _ { t + 1 } , \mathcal { A } _ { t + 1 } , z _ { t } ) ,\tag{23}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $z _ { t } \in \{ 0 , 1 \}$ i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>es w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>rans</sub>iti<sub>on reac</sub>h<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e.</sub> Mi<sub>n</sub>i<sub>-</sub>b<sub>a</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>es are ran</sub>d<sub>om</sub>l<sub>y samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> re<sub>p</sub><sup>l</sup>a<sub>y</sub> memor<sub>y</sub> <sup>f</sup>or networ<sup>k</sup> u<sub>p</sub><sup>d</sup>ates.

For each sampled transition, Double DQN decouples next-<sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> it<sub>s va</sub>l<sub>ue eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> S<sub>pec</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>on</sub>li<sub>ne ne</sub>t<sub>wor</sub>k <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>es</sub>t f<sub>eas</sub>ibl<sub>e ac</sub>ti<sub>on a</sub>t th<sub>e nex</sub>t <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e,</sub>

$$
u _ { t + 1 } ^ { \ast } = \arg \operatorname* { m a x } _ { u \in \boldsymbol { A } _ { t + 1 } } Q _ { \theta } \left( \mathbf { x } _ { t + 1 } , u \right) ,\tag{24}

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ereas</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t <sub>ne</sub>t<sub>wor</sub>k <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>o</sub> construct

$$
y _ { t } = r _ { t } + ( 1 - z _ { t } ) \gamma Q _ { \bar { \theta } } \left( \mathbf { x } _ { t + 1 } , u _ { t + 1 } ^ { * } \right) .\tag{25}
$$

I<sub>n our</sub> fi<sub>n</sub>it<sub>e-</sub>h<sub>or</sub>i<sub>zon</sub> f<sub>ormu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> $\gamma = 1$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>or</sub> $1 - z _ { t }$ <sub>removes</sub> b<sub>oo</sub>t<sub>s</sub>t<sub>rapp</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> f<sub>or</sub> t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l t<sub>rans</sub>iti<sub>ons.</sub>

Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>on</sub>li<sub>ne</sub> <sub>ne</sub>t<sub>wor</sub>k i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> <sub>m</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>m</sub>i<sub>z</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> H<sub>u</sub>b<sub>er</sub> l<sub>oss</sub> between the predicted action value and the DDQN tar<sub>g</sub>et:

$$
\mathcal { L } ( \boldsymbol { \theta } ) = \frac { 1 } { \left| \mathcal { M } \right| } \sum _ { i \in \mathcal { M } } \ell _ { \mathrm { H u b e r } } \left( Q _ { \theta } ( \mathbf { x } _ { i } , u _ { i } ) - y _ { i } \right) ,\tag{26}
$$

where M denotes a mini-batch sam<sub>p</sub>led from the re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub> memor<sub>y</sub>. The parameters θ are updated using Adam, while th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>arge</sub>t<sub>-ne</sub>t<sub>wor</sub>k <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>ers</sub> ¯θ <sub>are</sub> <sub>per</sub>i<sub>o</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>sync</sub>h<sub>ron</sub>i<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e on</sub>li<sub>ne ne</sub>t<sub>wor</sub>k<sub>.</sub>

T<sub>o</sub> im<sub>p</sub>r<sub>ove ea</sub>rl<sub>y-s</sub>t<sub>age exp</sub>l<sub>o</sub>r<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> r<sub>ep</sub>l<sub>ay</sub> m<sub>e</sub>m<sub>o</sub>r<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s</sub> initialized with a small number of trajectories generated by a deficit-based heuristic.
